    Thus $a$ and $b$ are fixed independently of the supports. They satisfy $b>8a$ and $c>16a$. The interaction bound $C_\mathrm{int}(a,c)=\frac{\pi}{2\kappa_{c,c/2}}(\frac c{8a}-1)$ from Proposition \ref{prop: loc of phi} satisfies
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{C_\mathrm{int}(a,c)}{1+|\beta|}
        >2\bigl(1+\norm H_{\exp(-c\cdot)}+2\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}\bigr).
    \end{align*}
    Both interactions $H+sV_{X_R}$ and $H+V_{X_R}+sV_{Y_R}$ therefore satisfy this bound uniformly in $s$, $X$, $Y$ and $R$.

    For $Z\in P_0(\Gamma)$ and $x\in Z$, the definition of the decay norm gives
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert V(Z)\rVert_{\exp(-b\cdot),x}
        \leq3\mathrm{e}^{b\mathrm{diam}(Z)}\lVert V(Z)\rVert.
    \end{align*}
    Proposition \ref{prop: loc of phi} consequently gives
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert \varPhi_\beta^{ \tau^{sV_{X_R}}} (V(Z)) \rVert_{\exp( - a \cdot), x}
        &\leq C_\varPhi(1+|\beta|)\norm{V(Z)}_{\exp(-b\cdot),x}\\
        &\leq 3C_\varPhi(1+|\beta|)\mathrm{e}^{b\mathrm{diam}(Z)}\lVert V(Z)\rVert,
    \end{align*}
    where $C_\varPhi>0$ depends only on the lattice and the fixed exponents $a,b,c$, and is uniform over the interactions satisfying the displayed norm bound. In particular, it is independent of $s$ and all supports and localization radii. Let $L\in\mathbb{N}$. For $x\in Z\subseteq X_R$, we have $B_L(x)\subseteq X_{R+L}$. Hence,
    \begin{align*}
        \bigl\lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{X_{R + L}}) \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{s V_{X_R}}} (V(Z)) \bigr\rVert
        &\leq 2 \bigl\lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_L(x)}) \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{ s V_{X_R} } } (V(Z)) \bigr\rVert
        \\
        &\leq 6C_\varPhi(1+|\beta|) \, \mathrm{e}^{-a  L} \, \mathrm{e}^{b \, \mathrm{diam}(Z)} \lVert V(Z)\rVert.
    \end{align*}
    The scalar centering term is annihilated by $1-\mathbb{E}_{X_{R+L}}$. Summing the last estimate over $Z\subseteq X_R$ and using $b<c$ therefore gives
    \begin{align*}
        &\bigl \lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{X_{R + L}}) \varPhi_\beta^{ \tau^{s V_{X_R}}} (V_{X_R} - \omega_{s V_{X_R}} (V_{X_R})) \bigr\rVert
        \\
        &\quad \leq 6C_\varPhi(1+|\beta|) \, \mathrm{e}^{-aL} \sum_{x \in X_R} \sum_{x \in Z \in P_0(\Gamma)} \mathrm{e}^{b \, \mathrm{diam}(Z)} \, \lVert V(Z) \rVert
        \\
        &\quad \leq 6C_\varPhi(1+|\beta|) \, |X_R| \, \mathrm{e}^{-aL} \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp(-c\cdot)}, \addtocounter{equation}{1}\tag{\theequation} \label{eq: proof generalized doc generator U}
    \end{align*}
    The uniform interaction bound also applies to the second QBP operator. Replacing $X_R$ by $Y_R$ and using the dynamics generated by $H+V_{X_R}+sV_{Y_R}$ yields
    \begin{align}
        \bigl\lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{Y_{R + L}}) \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{ V_{X_R} + s V_{Y_R}}} (V_{Y_R}) \bigr\rVert \leq 6C_\varPhi(1+|\beta|) \, |Y_R| \, \mathrm{e}^{-aL} \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp(- c \cdot)} \, , \label{eq: proof generalized doc generator W}
    \end{align}
    We now define the local approximations. Let $\eta_{X_R}^L$ be the Dyson series in Equation \eqref{eq: eta} with the QBP generator replaced by
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E}_{X_{R + L}} \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{\sigma V_{X_R}}} \bigl(V_{X_R}-\omega_{\sigma V_{X_R}}(V_{X_R})\bigr),
    \end{align*}
    and define $(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L$ by the corresponding replacement with
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E}_{Y_{R + L}} \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{V_{X_R}+\sigma V_{Y_R}}} \bigl(V_{Y_R}-\omega_{V_{X_R}+\sigma V_{Y_R}}(V_{Y_R})\bigr).
    \end{align*}
    By construction, $\eta_{X_R}^L\in\mathcal{A}_{X_{R+L}}$ and $(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L\in\mathcal{A}_{Y_{R+L}}$. To estimate their errors and norms, we use contractivity of $\varPhi_\beta$ and of the conditional expectations. In particular,
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV_{X_R}}}(V_{X_R})}
        &\leq\norm{V_{X_R}}\leq |X_R|\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)},\\
        \norm{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{V_{X_R}+sV_{Y_R}}}(V_{Y_R})}
        &\leq\norm{V_{Y_R}}\leq |Y_R|\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}.
    \end{align*}
    Centering increases these bounds by at most a factor of two. In each term of the difference of the Dyson series, we replace one generator at a time using \cite[Equation 34]{CMTW25} and use Equations \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc generator U} and \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc generator W}. Summing the series gives
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert\eta_{X_R}-\eta_{X_R}^L\rVert
        &\leq h^\prime (|\beta|) \, |X_R| \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp(-c \cdot)} \, \exp (2 |\beta| \, |X_R| \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp( - c \cdot)} - a L ) ,\nonumber\\
        \lVert\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R}-(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L\rVert
        &\leq h^\prime (|\beta|) \, |Y_R| \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp(-c \cdot)} \, \exp (2 |\beta| \, |Y_R| \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp( - c \cdot)} - a L) ,
    \end{align*}
    where $h^\prime(|\beta|)\coloneq3C_\varPhi|\beta|(1+|\beta|)$. The same generator norm bounds give
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert \eta_{X_R} \rVert, \, \lVert \eta_{X_R}^L \rVert &\leq \exp \bigl( 2 |\beta| \, |X_R| \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp( - c \cdot)} \bigr)
        \\
        \lVert \eta_{Y_R}^{X_R} \rVert , \, \lVert (\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L \rVert &\leq \exp \bigl(2 |\beta| \, |Y_R| \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp( - c \cdot)} \bigr) \addtocounter{equation}{1}\tag{\theequation} \label{eq: proof generalized doc qbp norms}
    \end{align*}
    We now estimate the effect on expectations of replacing both QBP operators by their local approximations. Using the error bound for the replaced factor and the norm bounds for the remaining factors gives
    \begin{align}
        &\big| \omega \bigl( \eta_{X_R} \eta_{Y_R}^{X_R} \, D \, (\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^* \eta_{X_R}^* \bigr) - \omega \bigl( \eta_{X_R}^L (\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L \, D \, ((\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L)^* (\eta_{X_R}^L)^* \bigr) \big| \nonumber
        \\
        &\quad \leq 2 h^\prime (|\beta|) \, (|X_R| + |Y_R| )  \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp(- c \cdot)} \, \exp \bigl( 6 |\beta| \, (|X_R| + |Y_R|) \, \lVert V \rVert_{\exp(-c \cdot)} - a L \bigr) \, \lVert D \rVert,
        \label{eq: proof generalized doc functional approximation}
    \end{align}
    for every $D\in\mathcal{A}_\Gamma$. This estimate determines the required width $L$. Choose $K>0$, depending only on $a$, the lattice and $\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}$, such that $aK>6C_0\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}$, and set
    \begin{align*}
        L\coloneq\left\lceil|\beta|K(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta\right\rceil.
    \end{align*}
    By Equation \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc size U W}, the exponent in the error bound is then negative with magnitude proportional to $(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta$. Absorbing the remaining factor $|X_R|+|Y_R|$ into half of this decay gives
    \begin{align*}
        &2 h^\prime (|\beta|)(|X_R|+|Y_R|)\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}
          \exp\bigl(6|\beta|(|X_R|+|Y_R|)\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}-aL\bigr)\\
        &\quad\leq C_2\exp\bigl(-\alpha(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta\bigr),
        \addtocounter{equation}{1}\tag{\theequation}\label{eq: proof generalized doc sequential qbp}
    \end{align*}
    where $\alpha\coloneq|\beta|(aK-6C_0\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)})/2>0$ and
    \begin{align*}
        C_2\coloneq1+\frac{h^\prime(|\beta|)C_0\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}}{\alpha}.
    \end{align*}
    These constants depend only on the lattice, $a,b,c$, $\beta$, $K$ and $\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}$, and are independent of both supports.

\subsubsection*{Normalizing the local approximation}

    The exact QBP expression is normalized, whereas replacing the QBP operators by local approximations need not preserve normalization. The same approximation bound controls this error: taking $D=1$ in Equation \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc functional approximation} and using Equation \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc sequential qbp} yields
    \begin{align*}
        |\omega\bigl(
        \eta_{X_R}^L(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L
        ((\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L)^*(\eta_{X_R}^L)^*
        \bigr)-1|
        \leq
        C_2\mathrm{e}^{-\alpha(1+d(X, Y))^\vartheta}. \addtocounter{equation}{1}\tag{\theequation} \label{eq: proof doc from doc+li normalization}
    \end{align*}
    Thus, for sufficiently large $d(X, Y)$,
    \begin{align}
        \frac12\leq \omega\bigl(
        \eta_{X_R}^L(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L
        ((\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L)^*(\eta_{X_R}^L)^*
        \bigr)\leq\frac32,
        \label{eq: proof generalized doc normalization}
    \end{align}
    We restrict to these distances and define the state $\varphi_L$ by
    \begin{align*}
        \varphi_L(D)
        \coloneq
        \frac{
        \omega\bigl(
        \eta_{X_R}^L(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L \, D \,
        ((\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L)^*(\eta_{X_R}^L)^*
        \bigr)}
        {\omega\bigl(
        \eta_{X_R}^L(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L
        ((\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L)^*(\eta_{X_R}^L)^*
        \bigr)}.
    \end{align*}
    Positivity and normalization give $\norm{\varphi_L}=1$. Thus, normalizing the local expression changes its value at $D$ by at most $\norm D$ times its normalization error. Together with Equations \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc functional approximation}--\eqref{eq: proof doc from doc+li normalization}, this gives
    \begin{align*}
        |\omega_{V_{X_R} + V_{Y_R}}(D) - \varphi_L(D)| \leq 2 C_2 \exp (-\alpha (1 + d(X,  Y))^\vartheta) \lVert D \rVert .
    \end{align*}
    Applying this estimate to $A B$, $A$ and $B$ yields
    \begin{gather}
        \big|
        \omega_{V_{X_R}+V_{Y_R}}(AB)
        -\omega_{V_{X_R}+V_{Y_R}}(A)\omega_{V_{X_R}+V_{Y_R}}(B)
        -\varphi_L(AB)+\varphi_L(A)\varphi_L(B)
        \big|\nonumber\\
        \leq
        6 C_2 \exp \bigl( -\alpha (1 + d(X, Y))^\vartheta \bigr)
        \lVert A\rVert\lVert B\rVert.
        \label{eq: proof generalized doc covariance approximation}
    \end{gather}

\subsubsection*{Using clustering of the initial state}

    It remains to bound the covariance in $\varphi_L$. The two local QBP operators have disjoint supports for large separation. Their evenness then allows us to move each operator past observables supported in the other region.
    By Lemma \ref{lem: dynamics is even}, the QBP generators are even. Proposition \ref{Ex+UniqueExpectation} shows that the conditional expectations preserve evenness. Therefore, $\eta_{X_R}^L \in \mathcal{A}_{X_{R+ L}}^+$ and $(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L \in \mathcal{A}_{Y_{R + L}}^+$. Since $R$ and $L$ grow sublinearly in $d(X,Y)$, we have
    \begin{align}
        d(X_{R + L}, Y_{R + L})
        \geq
        d(X, Y)-2R-2L
        \geq\frac{d(X, Y)}{2}
        \label{eq: proof generalized doc distance localized}
    \end{align}
    for all sufficiently large distances.

    To express the covariance of $\varphi_L$ in terms of the initial state, we use the operators
    \begin{align*}
        A_L&\coloneq\eta_{X_R}^L A(\eta_{X_R}^L)^*\in\mathcal{A}_{X_{R+L}},&
        P_L&\coloneq\eta_{X_R}^L(\eta_{X_R}^L)^*\in\mathcal{A}_{X_{R+L}},\\
        B_L&\coloneq(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L B((\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L)^*\in\mathcal{A}_{Y_{R+L}},&
        Q_L&\coloneq(\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L((\eta_{Y_R}^{X_R})^L)^*\in\mathcal{A}_{Y_{R+L}}.
    \end{align*}
    Here $P_L$ and $Q_L$ account for the normalization and are even. Moreover, $A_L$ is even whenever $A$ is even, and likewise for $B_L$. Commuting the local QBP operators past the observables in the other region gives
    \begin{align*}
        \varphi_L(AB)-\varphi_L(A)\varphi_L(B)
        =
        \frac{
        \omega(A_LB_L)\omega(P_LQ_L)
        -
        \omega(A_LQ_L)\omega(P_LB_L)}
        {\omega(P_LQ_L)^2}.
    \end{align*}
    The numerator would vanish if expectations of products between the two regions factorized. To estimate the error, we write it as four covariances of $\omega$:
    \begin{align*}
        &\omega(A_LB_L)\omega(P_LQ_L)-\omega(A_LQ_L)\omega(P_LB_L)\\
        &\quad=
        \bigl(\omega(A_LB_L)-\omega(A_L)\omega(B_L)\bigr)\omega(P_LQ_L)\\
        &\qquad+\omega(A_L)\omega(B_L)
                 \bigl(\omega(P_LQ_L)-\omega(P_L)\omega(Q_L)\bigr)\\
        &\qquad-\bigl(\omega(A_LQ_L)-\omega(A_L)\omega(Q_L)\bigr)\omega(P_LB_L)\\
        &\qquad-\omega(A_L)\omega(Q_L)
                 \bigl(\omega(P_LB_L)-\omega(P_L)\omega(B_L)\bigr).
    \end{align*}
    Each covariance is between the two enlarged supports, and one of its operators is even. Equation \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc qbp norms} gives
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{A_L}\norm{P_L}
        &\leq\exp(8|\beta|\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}|X_R|)\norm A,\\
        \norm{B_L}\norm{Q_L}
        &\leq\exp(8|\beta|\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}|Y_R|)\norm B.
    \end{align*}
    Taking absolute values in the four-term expansion therefore gives
    \begin{align*}
        &|\omega(A_LB_L)\omega(P_LQ_L)-\omega(A_LQ_L)\omega(P_LB_L)|\\
        &\quad\leq
        4|X_{R+L}|^{n_\mathrm{cor}}f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y_{R+L}|)
           \zeta_\mathrm{cor}(d(X_{R+L},Y_{R+L}))\\
        &\qquad\times
        \exp(8|\beta|\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}(|X_R|+|Y_R|))\norm A\norm B.
    \end{align*}
    By Equation \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc normalization}, the denominator is at least $1/4$. Hence
    \begin{gather*}
        |\varphi_L(AB)-\varphi_L(A)\varphi_L(B)| \addtocounter{equation}{1}\tag{\theequation}\label{eq: proof generalized doc localized covariance}
        \\
        \leq 16|X_{R+L}|^{n_\mathrm{cor}}f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y_{R+L}|)
           \zeta_\mathrm{cor}(d(X_{R+L},Y_{R+L})) \exp(8|\beta|\norm V_{\exp(-c\cdot)}(|X_R|+|Y_R|))\norm A\norm B.
    \end{gather*}
    We must still check that the support factors and QBP norms in this bound do not grow faster than the decay. This is where the choice of $\vartheta$ is used. By $D$-regularity, Equation \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc size U W} and the definition of $L$,
    \begin{align*}
        |X_{R + L}|+|Y_{R + L}| \leq C_\mathrm{vol} \, C_0 \, (2+|\beta|K)^D \, (1+d (X, Y))^{\vartheta(D+1)}.
    \end{align*}
    This estimate, the decay-of-correlations assumptions and Equation \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc distance localized} give
    \begin{align*}
        &|X_{R + L}|^{n_\mathrm{cor}} f_\mathrm{cor} (|Y_{R + L}|) \zeta_\mathrm{cor}( d(X_{R + L }, Y_{R + L}) )
        \\
        &\quad \leq C_3 \, (1 + d (X, Y))^{n_\mathrm{cor}(D+1)/(D+2)} \, \exp \bigl(C_3 (1 + d (X, Y))^{(D+1)/(D+2)} -\mu_\mathrm{cor} \, d (X, Y)/2 \bigr),
    \end{align*}
    where
    \begin{align*}
        C_3\coloneq C_\mathrm{cor}^2\mathrm{e}^{C_\mathrm{cor}}
        \bigl(C_\mathrm{vol} C_0(2+|\beta|K)^D\bigr)^{n_\mathrm{cor}+1}
    \end{align*}
    depends only on the lattice, $|\beta|K$, $C_\mathrm{cor}$ and $n_\mathrm{cor}$, not on $X$, $Y$, $R$ or $L$. Since $\vartheta(D+1)=(D+1)/(D+2)<1$, the logarithms of the support and QBP norm factors grow at most as $(1+d(X,Y))^{(D+1)/(D+2)}$ and $(1+d(X,Y))^{1/(D+2)}$, respectively. Both are sublinear in the distance of $X$ and $Y$ and can therefore be absorbed into half of the exponential clustering decay, with a threshold independent of the supports. Equation \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc localized covariance} gives
    \begin{align*}
        |\varphi_L(AB)-\varphi_L(A)\varphi_L(B)|
        &\leq16C_3\mathrm{e}^{-\mu_\mathrm{cor} d(X,Y)/4}\norm A\norm B.
    \end{align*}

\subsubsection*{Combining the errors}

    We now return to the covariance in $\omega_V$. There are three contributions: the LI error, the error from localizing the QBP operators, and the exponentially decaying terms from removing the connecting interactions and applying clustering. The factor $(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta$ in Equation \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc remove crossing} can be absorbed into half of its exponential decay. We may therefore take $\gamma=\min(\mu_\mathrm{cor},c)/4>0$, which depends only on the two decay rates. Combining Equations \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc LI}, \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc remove crossing}, \eqref{eq: proof generalized doc covariance approximation} and the preceding covariance bound gives
    \begin{align*}
        &|\omega_V(A B) - \omega_V(A) \, \omega_V(B)|
        \\
        & \quad \leq C_4 \, \lVert A\rVert \, \lVert B \rVert \, \bigl( \exp\bigl(C_\mathrm{LI}(1+|X|+|Y|)\bigr) \, \mathrm{e}^{- \mu_\mathrm{LI} R} + \mathrm{e}^{- \alpha (1 + d(X,  Y))^\vartheta} + \mathrm{e}^{- \gamma d (X, Y)} \bigr) .
    \end{align*}
    Here $C_4>0$ depends only on the lattice, $c$, $\beta$, the interaction norms and the stated clustering and LI data, and is independent of the supports. Since $R\geq1$,
    \begin{align*}
        R&\geq\frac12
          \left(\frac{(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta}{1+|X|+|Y|}\right)^{1/D}.
    \end{align*}
    Set $\kappa\coloneq\min (\mu_\mathrm{LI}/2,\alpha,\gamma)>0$. In this case,
    \begin{align*}
        \left(\frac{(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta}{1+|X|+|Y|}\right)^{1/D}
        \leq (1+d(X,Y))^{\vartheta/D}
        \leq \min ( (1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta,d(X,Y) )
    \end{align*}
    for all sufficiently large distances. Consequently,
    \begin{align*}
        \mathrm{e}^{-\mu_\mathrm{LI} R}
        &\leq \exp\left[-\kappa
          \left(\frac{(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta}{1+|X|+|Y|}\right)^{1/D}\right],\\
        \mathrm{e}^{-\alpha(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta}+\mathrm{e}^{-\gamma d(X,Y)}
        &\leq 2\exp\left[-\kappa
          \left(\frac{(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta}{1+|X|+|Y|}\right)^{1/D}\right]
    \end{align*}
    Using that $\vartheta = 1 / (D + 2)$, the combined estimate gives
    \begin{align*}
        |\omega_V(A B) - \omega_V(A) \, \omega_V(B)|
        &\leq 3C_4\exp\bigl(C_\mathrm{LI}(1+|X|+|Y|)\bigr)\lVert A \rVert \, \lVert B \rVert\\
        &\quad \times\exp\left( -\kappa
        \left(\frac{(1+d(X,Y))^{1/(D+2)}}{1+|X|+|Y|}\right)^{1/D}\right)
    \end{align*}
    whenever $1+|X|+|Y|\leq(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta$. If instead $(1+|X|+|Y|)>(1+d(X,Y))^\vartheta$, then
    \begin{align*}
        \left(\frac{(1+d(X,Y))^{1/(D+2)}}{1+|X|+|Y|}\right)^{1/D}<1,
    \end{align*}
    and the trivial covariance bound gives
    \begin{align*}
        |\omega_V(A B) - \omega_V(A) \, \omega_V(B)| &\leq 2\mathrm{e}^\kappa\exp\bigl(C_\mathrm{LI}(1+|X|+|Y|)\bigr)\lVert A \rVert \, \lVert B \rVert\\
        &\quad \times\exp\left(-\kappa
        \left(\frac{(1+d(X,Y))^{1/(D+2)}}{1+|X|+|Y|}\right)^{1/D}\right).
    \end{align*}
    Taking $C\geq\max (3C_4,2\mathrm{e}^\kappa)$ and increasing it to include the bounded distances excluded above proves the support-dependent estimate.

    The more transparent estimate follows from the weighted arithmetic--geometric mean inequality, which gives
    \begin{align*}
        (1+d(X,Y))^{1/((D+1)(D+2))}
        &\leq
        \left(\frac{(1+d(X,Y))^{1/(D+2)}}
        {1+|X|+|Y|}\right)^{1/D}
        +\frac{1+|X|+|Y|}{D+1}.
    \end{align*}
    Inserting this into the support-dependent estimate and increasing $C$ proves the bound in the theorem.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Let $A\in\mathcal{A}_Y$. For $\beta=0$, all KMS states are the tracial state and the claim becomes trivial. We henceforth assume $\beta\neq0$.

Increasing $C_\mathrm{cor}$ if necessary, we assume that $C_\mathrm{cor}\geq1$. By assumption, $m_\mathrm{cor}\geq\nu+Dn_\mathrm{cor}$. We first weaken the assumed correlation estimate, uniformly in $s$ and $M$, by calculating
\[
    C_\mathrm{cor}(1+r)^{-m_\mathrm{cor}}
    \leq C_\mathrm{cor}(1+r)^{-\nu-Dn_\mathrm{cor}}.
\]
Theorem \ref{thm: LPPL without doc} may therefore be applied with this weaker decay function. Its spatial factor satisfies
\[
 (1+r)^{Dn_\mathrm{cor}}C_\mathrm{cor}(1+r/2)^{-\nu-Dn_\mathrm{cor}} \leq
 2^{\nu+Dn_\mathrm{cor}}C_\mathrm{cor}(1+r)^{-\nu}.
\]
Thus $\omega_{sV_M}$ satisfies LPPL with decay $(1+r)^{-\nu}$ uniformly in $s$ and $M$. Since $\nu>D$, the uniform assertion of Theorem \ref{thm: li from lppl} gives LI with decay bounded by a constant times
\[
\sum_{k=\lceil r\rceil}^{\infty}(1+k)^{D-1-\nu}
\leq C(1+r)^{D-\nu}.
\]
Hence, Proposition \ref{prop: convergence from li} shows that, for any IAS $(\Lambda_n)_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ and $V_n=V_{\Lambda_n}$,
\begin{align*}
    \sup_{s\in[0,1]}|\omega_{sV_n}(B)-\omega_{sV}(B)|\longrightarrow0,
    \qquad B\in\mathcal{A}_\Gamma.
\end{align*}
Taking this limit in the covariance inequality for two local observables shows that $\omega_{sV}$ satisfies decay of correlations with the same constants as $\omega_{s V_n}$.

We start by showing the continuity claim. To this end, we first observe the following elementary bound. For each $Z\in P_0(\Gamma)$, we choose $z=\mathrm C(Z)$ the center of $Z$. By Lemma \ref{lem: decay of correlations for A_infty} and Proposition \ref{prop: loc of phi}, with the clustering bound just obtained, we have that
\begin{align*}
    &\bigl|\omega_{sV_n}\bigl(\{
       \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV_n}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV_n}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr)\bigr|\\
    &\quad\leq C\norm A f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y|)
        (1+d(z,Y))^{-\nu}
        \norm{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV_n}}(V(Z))}_{\nu, z}\\
    &\quad\leq
        Ch(|\beta|)\norm A f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y|)
        \frac{(1+\mathrm{diam} Z)^\nu\norm{V(Z)}}{(1+d(z ,Y))^\nu}.
        \addtocounter{equation}{1}\tag{\theequation}\label{eq: extensive common majorant}
\end{align*}
Here $h$ is the polynomial from Proposition \ref{prop: loc of phi} and $C > 0$ is a constant independent of $A$, $V$, $Y$, $z$ and $\beta$. The same bound holds for $\omega_{sV}$ and $\tau^{sV}$. The right-hand side is summable in $Z$, since
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{Z\in P_0(\Gamma)}
       \frac{(1+\mathrm{diam} Z)^\nu\norm{V(Z)}}{(1+d(\mathrm C(Z),Y))^\nu}
    &\leq\norm V_\nu\sum_{x\in\Gamma}(1+d(x,Y))^{-\nu}\\
    &\leq C_\mathrm{sur}|Y|\norm V_\nu
       \sum_{r=0}^\infty(1+r)^{D-1-\nu}<\infty.
\end{align*}


To show continuity, let $s,t\in[0,1]$. Proposition \ref{prop: QBP differential equation} gives
\begin{align*}
    |\omega_{sV}(A)-\omega_{tV}(A)|
    &\leq |s-t|\limsup_{n\to\infty}
        \sup_{u\in[0,1]}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} u}\omega_{uV_n}(A)\right|\\
    &\leq\frac{|\beta|}{2}|s-t|\limsup_{n\to\infty}
        \sup_{u\in[0,1]}\sum_{Z\subseteq\Lambda_n}
        \bigl|\omega_{uV_n}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{uV_n}}(V(Z)-\omega_{uV_n}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr)\bigr|\\
    &\leq |s-t|\,g(|\beta|)\norm A\,|Y|f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y|)\norm V_\nu,
\end{align*}
where we may take the polynomial
\begin{align*}
    g(|\beta|)=\frac{|\beta|}{2}CC_\mathrm{sur} h(|\beta|)
                 \sum_{r=0}^\infty(1+r)^{D-1-\nu}.
\end{align*}
Here we used Equation \eqref{eq: extensive common majorant} and the preceding sum. This proves the continuity claim.

It remains to identify the derivative of the limiting state. For every finite patch, Proposition \ref{prop: QBP differential equation} gives
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} s}\omega_{sV_n}(A)
    &=-\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{Z\subseteq\Lambda_n}
       \omega_{sV_n}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV_n}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV_n}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr).
\end{align*}
We show that the right-hand side converges uniformly in $s\in[0,1]$ to the corresponding infinite-volume sum. We first fix $Z\in P_0(\Gamma)$ and prove uniform convergence of its summand. Lemma \ref{lem: str conv time evol} gives convergence of the dynamics uniformly in $s$ on compact time intervals. Splitting the integral defining $\varPhi_\beta$ at $T>0$, we find
\begin{align*}
    &\sup_{s\in[0,1]}
      \norm{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV_n}}(V(Z))
                 -\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z))}\\
    &\quad\leq
      \sup_{\substack{s\in[0,1]\\|t|\leq T}}
      \norm{\tau_{-t}^{sV_n}(V(Z))-\tau_{-t}^{sV}(V(Z))}
      +2\norm{V(Z)}\int_{|t|>T}f_\beta(t)\,\mathrm{d} t.
\end{align*}
It follows that
\begin{align*}
    \sup_{s\in[0,1]}
      \norm{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV_n}}(V(Z))
                 -\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z))}
    \longrightarrow0.
\end{align*}
Together with the uniform convergence of the states, this gives
\begin{align*}
    &\sup_{s\in[0,1]}
       \norm{\{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV_n}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV_n}(V(Z))),A\}
              -\{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV}(V(Z))),A\}}\\
    &\quad\leq2\norm A\sup_{s\in[0,1]}
       \norm{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV_n}}(V(Z))-\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z))}\\
    &\qquad+2\norm A\sup_{s\in[0,1]}
       |\omega_{sV_n}(V(Z))-\omega_{sV}(V(Z))|
       \longrightarrow0.
\end{align*}

We still have to replace the state acting on this observable. The continuity assertion of Lemma \ref{lem: str conv time evol}, together with the same integral estimate, shows that $s\mapsto\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z))$ is norm-continuous. Since $s\mapsto\omega_{sV}(V(Z))$ is continuous, the set
\begin{align*}
    \bigl\{\{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV}(V(Z))),A\}:s\in[0,1]\bigr\}
\end{align*}
is compact. For $\epsilon>0$, choose an $\epsilon$-net $D_1,\ldots,D_N$ for this set. Since both states have norm one,
\begin{align*}
    &\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\bigl|(\omega_{sV_n}-\omega_{sV})
          \bigl(\{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr)\bigr|\\
    &\quad\leq2\epsilon+
        \max_{1\leq j\leq N}\sup_{s\in[0,1]}
            |(\omega_{sV_n}-\omega_{sV})(D_j)|.
\end{align*}
The last term converges to zero by the uniform convergence of the states. First letting $n\to\infty$ and then $\epsilon\to0$, we conclude that, for every fixed $Z$,
\begin{align*}
    &\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\Bigl|
       \omega_{sV_n}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV_n}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV_n}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr)-\omega_{sV}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr)
       \Bigr|\longrightarrow0.
\end{align*}

We regard a summand in the finite-patch derivative as zero when $Z\nsubseteq\Lambda_n$. For each fixed $Z$, this does not affect the preceding limit, since $Z\subseteq\Lambda_n$ for all sufficiently large $n$. Equation \eqref{eq: extensive common majorant} bounds all summands by the same summable expression, uniformly in $s$ and $n$. Dominated convergence therefore gives uniform convergence of the finite-patch derivatives to
\begin{align*}
    -\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{Z\in P_0(\Gamma)}
       \omega_{sV}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr).
\end{align*}
The derivatives for finite patches are continuous by Proposition \ref{prop: QBP differential equation}, Lemma \ref{lem: continuity of phi} and norm continuity of bounded perturbations. Their uniform limit is therefore continuous. For any $s_0 \in [0,1]$, the finite-patch identity
\begin{align*}
    \omega_{sV_n}(A)-\omega_{s_0 V_n}(A)
    =\int_{s_0}^s\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} u}\omega_{uV_n}(A)\,\mathrm{d} u
\end{align*}
converges uniformly to
\begin{align*}
    \omega_{sV}(A)-\omega_{s_0 V}(A)
    &=-\frac{\beta}{2}\int_{s_0}^s\sum_{Z\in P_0(\Gamma)}
       \omega_{uV}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{uV}}(V(Z)-\omega_{uV}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d} u.
\end{align*}
The integrand is continuous. Hence, the fundamental theorem of calculus shows that $s\mapsto\omega_{sV}(A)$ is continuously differentiable and
\begin{align*}
    \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d} s}\omega_{sV}(A)
    &=-\frac{\beta}{2}\sum_{Z\in P_0(\Gamma)}
       \omega_{sV}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr).
\end{align*}
Finally, absolute convergence and norm convergence of $V_x=\sum_{Z\in R_x}V(Z)$ give
\begin{align*}
    \sum_{Z\in P_0(\Gamma)}
       \omega_{sV}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr) &=
       \sum_{x\in\Gamma}\sum_{Z\in R_x}
       \omega_{sV}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V(Z)-\omega_{sV}(V(Z))),A\}\bigr)\\
    &=\sum_{x\in\Gamma}
       \omega_{sV}\bigl(\{
          \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sV}}(V_x-\omega_{sV}(V_x)),A\}\bigr).
\end{align*}
This proves the stated formula, including absolute and uniform convergence in $s$.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem: impl is entire}
    Let $(\mathcal{A}, \tau)$ be a $C^*$-dynamical system, $A \in \mathcal{A}_\mathrm{ent}^\tau$ and $\omega$ a $(\tau, \beta)$-KMS state. Let $(\mathcal{H}, \pi, \Omega)$ be the associated GNS triple and $H$ the Hamiltonian that implements the dynamics in the GNS Hilbert space. It holds that the map
    \begin{align*}
        z \mapsto \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i} z H} \, \pi(A) \, \Omega
    \end{align*}
    is entire with respect to the norm of $\mathcal{H}$. Moreover, it holds that
    \begin{align*}
        \pi(\tau_z(A)) \, \Omega = \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i} z H} \, \pi(A) \, \Omega \, ,
    \end{align*}
    for all $z \in \mathbb{C}$.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
We first notice that since $\omega$ is $\tau$ invariant, it holds that for $t \in \mathbb{R}$
    \begin{align*}
        \pi( \tau_t(A) ) \, \Omega = \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i} t H} \pi(A) \, \Omega \, .
    \end{align*}
    Since $A \in \mathcal{A}_\mathrm{ent}^\tau$ we find that the left-hand side is differentiable. Hence, by the definition of the generator of unitary groups, it holds that
    \begin{align*}
        \pi \bigl( \mathcal{L} (\tau_t (A)) \bigr) \, \Omega = H \, \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i} t H} \, \pi(A) \, \Omega \, .
    \end{align*}
    Again, the left-hand side is differentiable, and we can iterate this process to find
    \begin{align*}
        \pi(\mathcal{L}^n (A)) \, \Omega = H^n \, \pi(A) \, \Omega \, ,
    \end{align*}
    for every $n\in\mathbb{N}$. In particular, $\pi(A)\Omega\in D(H^n)$. Fix $R>0$. Cauchy's estimate for the entire map $z\mapsto\tau_z(A)$ gives
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{\norm{H^k\pi(A)\Omega}_{\mathcal{H}}}{k!}
        &\leq\frac{\norm{\mathcal{L}^k(A)}}{k!}
        \leq\frac{\sup_{|z|=2R}\norm{\tau_z(A)}}{(2R)^k}.
    \end{align*}
    Now let $N \geq M$ for $N$, $M \in \mathbb{N}$. Using this bound we find that
    \begin{align*}   
        \sup_{|z| \leq R} \bigl \lVert \sum_{k=0}^{N}\frac{(\mathrm{i} z)^k}{k!}H^k\pi(A)\Omega - \sum_{k=0}^{M}\frac{(\mathrm{i} z)^k}{k!}H^k\pi(A)\Omega \bigr \rVert
        &\leq 
        \sup_{|z|\leq R}\bigl\|
             \sum_{k=M+1}^{N}\frac{(\mathrm{i} z)^k}{k!}H^k\pi(A)\Omega
             \bigr\|_{\mathcal{H}}
             \\
        &\leq\sup_{|z|=2R}\norm{\tau_z(A)}
                   \sum_{k=M+1}^\infty2^{-k}.
    \end{align*}
    Hence the series converges locally uniformly to an entire vector-valued function. To show that it equals the exponential function defined via the spectral theorem let $\mathbf1_{[-L,L]}(H)$ be the spectral projection of $H$ onto $[-L,L]$. Then
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{\mathrm{e}^{R|H|}\mathbf1_{[-L,L]}(H)\pi(A)\Omega}_{\mathcal{H}}
        &\leq\sum_{k=0}^\infty\frac{R^k}{k!}
                        \norm{H^k\pi(A)\Omega}_{\mathcal{H}}\\
        &\leq2\sup_{|z|=2R}\norm{\tau_z(A)}.
    \end{align*}
    The spectral theorem and monotone convergence as $L \to \infty$ show that $\pi(A)\Omega\in D(\mathrm{e}^{R|H|})$. The spectral theorem and dominated convergence therefore give, for $|z|\leq R$,
    \begin{align*}
        \mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i} zH}\pi(A)\Omega
        &=\sum_{k=0}^\infty\frac{(\mathrm{i} z)^k}{k!}H^k\pi(A)\Omega\\
        &=\pi\left(\sum_{k=0}^\infty\frac{(\mathrm{i} z)^k}{k!}\mathcal{L}^k(A)\right)\Omega
        =\pi(\tau_z(A))\Omega.
    \end{align*}
    Since $R$ was arbitrary, both assertions follow.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem: continuity of phi}
    Let $(\mathcal{A}, \tau)$ be a $C^*$-dynamical system, $P = P^* \in \mathcal{A}$ and $s \in [0, 1]$. Denote by $\tau^{s P}$ the perturbed dynamics. Then the map 
    \begin{align*}
        s \mapsto \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{s P}} (A) \, ,   
    \end{align*}
    where $\varPhi_\beta$ is defined in Equation \eqref{def: Phi}, is continuous (in norm) for any $A \in \mathcal{A}$.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
For $\beta=0$ the map is constant because $\varPhi_0=\mathrm{id}$. Assume $\beta\neq0$. Let $s$ and $s_0 \in [0, 1]$. We calculate
    \begin{align*}
        \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{s P}} (A) - \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{s_0 P}} (A) &= \int_\mathbb{R} \mathrm{d} t \, f_\beta (t) \, \bigl( \tau_{- t}^{s P} (A) - \tau_{- t}^{s_0 P} (A) \bigr) 
        \\
        &= \int_\mathbb{R} \mathrm{d} t \, f_\beta (t) \, \bigl( (\tau_{- t}^{s_0 P})^{(s - s_0) P} (A) - \tau_{- t}^{s_0 P} (A) \bigr) \, ,
    \end{align*}
    where the second equality follows by the uniqueness of the dynamics generated by a given derivation. Duhamel's formula gives
    \[
        \norm{\tau_{-t}^{sP}(A)-\tau_{-t}^{s_0P}(A)}
        \leq 2|t|\,|s-s_0| \, \norm P \, \norm A.
    \]
    Hence,
    \[
        \norm{\varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{sP}}(A)-
        \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{s_0P}}(A)}
        \leq2|s-s_0|\norm P\norm A
        \int_\mathbb{R} |t|f_\beta(t)\,\mathrm{d} t
        \longrightarrow0.
    \]
    This proves the statement.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{Ex+UniqueExpectation}
    For each $M \subseteq \Gamma$ there exists a unique linear map 
    \begin{align*}
        \mathbb{E}_M : \mathcal{A}_\Gamma \to \mathcal{A}_M\,,
    \end{align*}
    called the conditional expectation with respect to $\omega^\mathrm{tr}$, such that
    \begin{equation*}
      \forall A \in \mathcal{A}_\Gamma \; \; \forall B \in \mathcal{A}_M \, :\quad \omega^{\mathrm{tr}}(A B) = \omega^{\mathrm{tr}} (\mathbb{E}_M(A) B) \,.
    \end{equation*}
    It is unital, positive and has the properties 
    \begin{eqnarray*}
        \forall M \subseteq \Gamma \;\; \forall A, C \in \mathcal{A}_M \;\; \forall B \in \mathcal{A}_\Gamma \, : &&  \mathbb{E}_M (A\, B\, C) = A \, \mathbb{E}_M(B) \, C 
        \\[1mm]
        \forall M_1, M_2 \subseteq \Gamma \, : && \mathbb{E}_{M_1} \circ \mathbb{E}_{M_2} = \mathbb{E}_{M_1 \cap M_2} \,
        \\[1mm]
        \forall M \subseteq \Gamma \,: && \mathbb{E}_M \mathcal{A}_\Gamma^+ \subseteq \mathcal{A}_\Gamma^+
        \\[1mm]
        \forall M \subseteq \Gamma \; \;  \forall A \in \mathcal{A}_\Gamma\,:&&  \lVert \mathbb{E}_M(A) \rVert \leq \lVert A \rVert \,.
    \end{eqnarray*}
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
This statement was proved for $\Gamma=\mathbb{Z}^D$ in \cite[Theorem 4.7]{AM03}. The proof uses only the CAR structure and the tracial state, and therefore applies to the present lattice.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem: derivations}
      Let $\alpha > 0$ and $\varPhi \in  \mathcal{P}_{D +\alpha}$. It holds that $\mathcal{A}_{D + \alpha} \subseteq D(\mathcal{L}_{\varPhi})$. For all $ A\in \mathcal{A}_{D + \alpha}$ the sums
    \[\sum_{M\in P_0(\Gamma)} [\,\varPhi(M), \,A\,] \quad \text{and} \quad \sum_{x \in \Gamma} [\, \varPhi_x, \, A \, ] \]
    converge absolutely and
    \[ \mathcal{L}_{\varPhi } \, A 
    \;=\;  \sum_{M\in P_0(\Gamma)} [\,\varPhi(M)  , \,A\,]
    \;=\;  \sum_{x\in \Gamma} [\, \varPhi_x, \, A \, ] 
    \, .
    \]
    Furthermore, there is a constant $c_\alpha$, independent of $\varPhi$ and $A$, such that for all~$x\in \Gamma$
    \[
    \norm{ \mathcal{L}_{\varPhi } \, A }_{\alpha,x} \leq c_\alpha \, \norm{\varPhi}_{D + \alpha} \, \norm{A}_{D + \alpha, x}
    \,.
    \]
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Fix $x\in\Gamma$ and set
    \begin{align*}
        A_0=\mathbb{E}_{B_1(x)}A,\qquad
        A_j=\mathbb{E}_{B_{2^j}(x)}A-\mathbb{E}_{B_{2^{j-1}}(x)}A,\quad j\geq1.
    \end{align*}
    Then $A=\sum_{j=0}^\infty A_j$ in norm and $A_j\in\mathcal{A}_{B_{2^j}(x)}$. By surface regularity and the definition of the decay norm, we find that
    \[
        \norm{A_j} \, |B_{2^j}(x)|
        \leq 2^{2D+\alpha+1}C_\mathrm{vol}\,2^{-j\alpha}\norm A_{D+\alpha,x},
    \]
    for all $j\geq0$. We also have the bound
    \[
        \sum_M\norm{[\varPhi(M),A_j]}
        \leq2\norm{A_j} \, |B_{2^j}(x)| \, \norm\varPhi_{D+\alpha}.
    \]
    Summing over $j$ proves absolute convergence of the commutator sum. The local partial sums converge to $A$ and their derivations converge in norm. Closedness of $\mathcal{L}_\varPhi$ therefore gives $A\in D(\mathcal{L}_\varPhi)$ and the first identity. Absolute convergence also permits regrouping the terms by their centers and proves the second identity.

    To bound the decay norm, fix $r\geq4$. If $2^j\leq r/2$, every nonzero commutator contributing to
    $(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_r(x)})\mathcal{L}_\varPhi A_j$ has $M\cap B_{2^j}(x)\neq\emptyset$ and $M\nsubseteq B_r(x)$, hence $\mathrm{diam} M\geq r/2$. Thus
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_r(x)})\mathcal{L}_\varPhi A}
        &\leq4(1+r/2)^{-D-\alpha}\norm\varPhi_{D+\alpha}
              \sum_{2^j\leq r/2}\norm{A_j} \, |B_{2^j}(x)| + 4 \norm\varPhi_{D+\alpha}
              \sum_{2^j>r/2}\norm{A_j} \, |B_{2^j}(x)|\\
        &\leq 2^{2D+\alpha+3}C_\mathrm{vol}\norm\varPhi_{D+\alpha}\norm A_{D+\alpha,x}
          \left((1+r/2)^{-D-\alpha}\sum_{j=0}^\infty2^{-j\alpha}
                +\sum_{2^j>r/2}2^{-j\alpha}\right)\\
        &\leq c_\alpha(1+r)^{-\alpha}
                     \norm\varPhi_{D+\alpha}\norm A_{D+\alpha,x}.
    \end{align*}
    The geometric sums converge since $\alpha>0$. The norm bound above covers $r<4$, and increasing $c_\alpha$ proves the claim.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:commutator bound}
    Let $F$ be a decay function, $x \in \Gamma$, $Y \in P_0 (\Gamma)$, and $A\in \mathcal{A}_Y$, $B \in \mathcal{A}_{F}$, where one of them is even. It holds that
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{[A,B]} \leq \frac{2}{F(1)} F(d(x, Y)) \,\norm{A} \, \norm{B}_{F, x}\, .
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Let $x \in \Gamma$, $Y \in P_0 (\Gamma)$ and $A \in \mathcal{A}_Y$, $B \in \mathcal{A}_F$. First, we notice that the statement is trivial for $x \in Y$. Therefore, we only consider the case $d(x, Y) \geq 1$. We define $\gamma \coloneq d(x, Y) - 1$ and calculate
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert [A, B] \rVert \leq \lVert [A, \mathbb{E}_{B_{\gamma} (x)} B] \rVert + \lVert [A, (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_\gamma (x)} ) \, B] \rVert \, .
    \end{align*}
    The first term vanishes by definition of $\gamma$. Hence, we find
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert [A, B] \rVert &\leq \lVert [A, (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_\gamma (x)} ) \, B] \rVert 
        \\
        & \leq 2 \lVert A \rVert \, \lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_\gamma (x)}) \, B \rVert 
        \\
        &\leq 2 F (\gamma) \, \lVert A \rVert \, \lVert B \rVert_{F, x} \, . 
    \end{align*}
    By convexity of the logarithm of $F$ we can bound $F(\gamma ) \leq \frac{1}{F(1)} F(d(x, Y))$. This estimate yields the claim.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem: continuity decay norm}
    Let $F$ be a decay function and $A$, $B \in \mathcal{A}_F$. It follows that
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert A B \rVert_{F, x} \leq 2 \, \lVert A \rVert_{F, x} \, \lVert B \rVert_{F, x} \, , 
    \end{align*}
    for all $x \in \Gamma$. Moreover, for each $x\in\Gamma$, the set of all $A\in\mathcal{A}_\Gamma$ with $\norm A_{F,x}<\infty$ is complete with respect to $\norm{\cdot}_{F,x}$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Fix $x\in\Gamma$ and $k\in\mathbb{N}_0$. We calculate
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{AB-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}(AB)} &=\norm{(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)})
        \bigl(AB-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}(A)\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}(B)\bigr)}\\
        &\quad\leq2\norm{AB-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}(A)\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}(B)}\\
        &\quad\leq2\norm{A-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}(A)}\norm B
        +2\norm A\norm{B-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}(B)}.
    \end{align*}
    After division by $F(k)$ and taking the supremum over $k$, this gives
    \[
    \begin{aligned}
        \norm{AB}_{F,x}
        &\leq\norm A\norm B
        +2(\norm A_{F,x}-\norm A)\norm B
        +2\norm A(\norm B_{F,x}-\norm B)\\
        &\leq2\norm A_{F,x}\norm B_{F,x}.
    \end{aligned}
    \]
    For completeness, let $(A_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be Cauchy in
    $\norm{\cdot}_{F,x}$. It is Cauchy in the $C^*$-norm and hence converges
    in norm to some $A\in\mathcal{A}_\Gamma$. For every $n$ and $k$,
    \[
    \begin{aligned}
      \frac{\norm{(A-A_n)-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}(A-A_n)}}{F(k)}
      &=\lim_{m\to\infty}
      \frac{\norm{(A_m-A_n)-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}(A_m-A_n)}}{F(k)}\\
      &\leq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\norm{A_m-A_n}_{F,x}.
    \end{aligned}
    \]
    The right-hand side is independent of $k$. Taking the supremum
    over $k$, and also using
    $\norm{A-A_n}\leq\limsup_{m\to\infty}\norm{A_m-A_n}_{F,x}$, gives
    \[
    \norm{A-A_n}_{F,x}
    \leq2\limsup_{m\to\infty}\norm{A_m-A_n}_{F,x}.
    \]
    Letting $n\to\infty$ proves that
    $\norm{A-A_n}_{F,x}\to0$.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem: decay of correlations for A_infty}
    Let $\beta \in \mathbb{R}$ and $\omega$ be a $(\tau, \beta )$-KMS state on $\mathcal{A}_\Gamma$. Let $F$ be some decay function. Assume that $\omega$ satisfies decay of correlations with respect to $\zeta_\mathrm{cor}$, $f_\mathrm{cor}$, and $n_\mathrm{cor}$. Assume that 
    \begin{align*}
        F(r) \leq C (1 + r)^{D \, n_\mathrm{cor}} \, \zeta_{\mathrm{cor}} (r) \, , \qquad r \geq 0 \, , 
    \end{align*}
    for some constant $C > 0$. Then there exists another constant $C^\prime > 0$ only depending on $\Gamma$, $n_\mathrm{cor}$, $C$ and $F(1)^{-1}$ such that
    \begin{align*}
        |\omega(A B ) - \omega(A) \, \omega(B)| \leq C^\prime \, \lVert A \rVert \, \lVert B \rVert_{F, x} \, f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y|) \, (1 + d(x, Y))^{D \, n_\mathrm{cor}} \, \zeta_{\mathrm{cor}} (d(x, Y) / 2 ) \, ,
    \end{align*}
    for all $Y \in P_0 (\Gamma)$, $x \in \Gamma$, and $A \in \mathcal{A}_Y$, $B \in \mathcal{A}_F$, where one of them is even.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Let $A\in\mathcal{A}_Y$ and $B\in\mathcal{A}_F$ be such that one of them is even. If $d(x, Y)=0$, then the assumption $F(0)=1\leq C\zeta_\mathrm{cor}(0)$ and $f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y|)\geq1$ give
    \begin{align*}
        |\omega(AB)-\omega(A)\omega(B)|
        &\leq2\norm A\norm B\\
        &\leq2C\norm A\norm B_{F,x}f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y|)
        (1+d(x, Y))^{Dn_\mathrm{cor}}\zeta_\mathrm{cor}(d(x, Y)/2).
    \end{align*}
    We therefore assume $d(x, Y)\geq1$ and set $k=\lfloor d(x, Y)/2\rfloor$. We calculate
    \begin{align*}
        |\omega(AB)-\omega(A)\omega(B)|
        &\leq |\omega(A(B-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B))
        -\omega(A)\omega(B-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B)|\\
        &\quad+|\omega(A\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B)
        -\omega(A)\omega(\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B)|.
    \end{align*}
    Since $k=\lfloor d(x, Y)/2\rfloor$, convexity of $\log F$ gives $F(k) \leq F(d(x, Y)/2) / F(1)$. The first term is therefore bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        &|\omega(A(B-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B))
        -\omega(A)\omega(B-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B)|\\
        &\quad\leq2F(k)\norm A\norm B_{F,x}\\
        &\quad\leq\frac{2C}{F(1)}\norm A\norm B_{F,x}
        f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y|)(1+d(x, Y))^{Dn_\mathrm{cor}}\zeta_\mathrm{cor}(d(x, Y)/2).
    \end{align*}
    Furthermore, $B_k(x)\cap Y=\emptyset$ and $d(B_k(x),Y) \geq d(x, Y)/2$. The conditional expectation preserves evenness. Hence one of
    $\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B$ and $A$ is even, and these two operators commute because their supports are disjoint. Applying decay of correlations gives
    \begin{align*}
        |\omega(A\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B)
        -\omega(A)\omega(\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B)|  &=|\omega((\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B)A)
        -\omega(\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B)\omega(A)|\\
        &\leq\norm{\mathbb{E}_{B_k(x)}B}\norm A
        |B_k(x)|^{n_\mathrm{cor}}f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y|)
        \zeta_\mathrm{cor}(d(B_k(x),Y))\\
        &\leq C_\mathrm{vol}^{n_\mathrm{cor}}\norm A\norm B_{F,x}
        f_\mathrm{cor}(|Y|)(1+d(x, Y))^{Dn_\mathrm{cor}}\zeta_\mathrm{cor}(d(x, Y)/2).
    \end{align*}
    Adding both estimates proves the claim.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem: dynamics is even}
    Consider the $C^*$-algebra $\mathcal{A}_\Gamma$ and let $\tau^P$ be the dynamics generated by the derivation $\mathcal{L}_\varPhi + \mathcal{L}_P$, where $\varPhi \in \mathcal{P}_{D+\alpha}$ with $\alpha>D$ and $P = P^* \in \mathcal{A}_\Gamma^+$. Then the dynamics maps even observables to even observables, that is
    \begin{align*}
        \tau_t^P \, \mathcal{A}_\Gamma^+ \subseteq \mathcal{A}_\Gamma^+ \, ,
    \end{align*}
    for all $t \in \mathbb{R}$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
First, notice that $\Theta^2 = \mathrm{id}$. Let $A \in \mathcal{A}_0$ and calculate
    \begin{align*}
        - \mathrm{i} \, \partial_t \Theta \, \tau_t^P  \, \Theta (A) &= \Theta  \, \tau_t^P (\mathcal{L}_\varPhi (\Theta (A)) + \mathcal{L}_{P} (\Theta (A)) )
        \\
        &= \Theta \, \tau_t^P \bigl( \sum_{x \in \Gamma} [\varPhi_x, \Theta (A)]  + [P, \Theta (A)] \bigr)
        \\
        &= \Theta \, \tau_t^P \, \Theta (\mathcal{L}_\varPhi(A) + \mathcal{L}_P(A) ) \, ,
    \end{align*}
    where we have used Lemma \ref{lem: derivations}. By uniqueness of the dynamics that is generated by $\mathcal{L}_\varPhi + \mathcal{L}_P$ it follows that $\Theta \, \tau^P \, \Theta = \tau^P$.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem: str conv time evol}
    Let $H,V\in\mathcal{P}_F$ for a decay function $F$, and assume that $\mathcal{L}_{H+sV}$ generates a dynamics $\tau^{sV}$ for every $s\in[0,1]$. For an IAS $(\Lambda_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$, we set $V_n  \coloneq V_{\Lambda_n}$. Then, for every $A\in\mathcal{A}_\Gamma$ and $T>0$,
    \begin{align*}
        \lim_{n \to \infty} \sup_{\substack {s \in [0,1]}} \sup_{|t| \leq T} \norm{ \tau_t^{s V_n} (A) - \tau_t^{sV} (A)} = 0.
    \end{align*}
    Moreover, $s\mapsto\tau_t^{sV}(A)$ is norm-continuous, uniformly for $|t|\leq T$.
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
The algebra $\mathcal{A}_0$ is a core for $\mathcal{L}_{H+sV}$ by the definition of this derivation as a closure. For $A\in\mathcal{A}_Y$ and $s_n\to s$, we calculate
    \begin{align*}
        &\norm{(\mathcal{L}_H+s_n\mathcal{L}_{V_n}-\mathcal{L}_{H+sV})A} \leq
        2 \norm A \, |s_n-s| \sum_{\substack{Z \in P_0 (\Gamma) \\ Z \cap Y \neq \emptyset }} \norm{V(Z)}
        +2\norm A\sum_{\substack{Z \in P_0 (\Gamma) \\ Z\cap Y\neq\emptyset,  Z \nsubseteq \Lambda_n}}\norm{V(Z)}. \addtocounter{equation}{1}\tag{\theequation} \label{eq: proof lemma convergence}
    \end{align*}
    We have the elementary bound on the sum
    \begin{align*}
        \sum_{Z\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\norm{V(Z)}
        \leq\sum_{y\in Y}\sum_{Z\ni y}\norm{V(Z)}
        \leq |Y|\norm V_F<\infty.
    \end{align*}
    Hence, the first term in Equation \eqref{eq: proof lemma convergence} tends to zero as $n \to \infty$. Also the second term in this equation vanishes in the limit $n \to \infty$ by dominated convergence. All the time evolutions are isometries. The first Trotter--Kato approximation theorem \cite[Theorem III.4.8]{EN00}, applied to $\pm\mathrm{i}(\mathcal{L}_H+s_n\mathcal{L}_{V_n})$ and $\pm\mathrm{i}\mathcal{L}_{H+sV}$, yields
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{|t|\leq T}\norm{\tau_t^{s_nV_n}(A)-\tau_t^{sV}(A)}\longrightarrow0.
    \end{align*}
This convergence holds for every $A\in\mathcal{A}_\Gamma$.
    We first establish the continuity in $s$ which will be used below. For $A\in\mathcal{A}_Y$ and $s_n\to s$ we have
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{(\mathcal{L}_{H+s_nV}-\mathcal{L}_{H+sV})A}
        &\leq2|s_n-s|\norm A
        \sum_{Z\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\norm{V(Z)}\\
        &\leq2|s_n-s|\,|Y|\norm V_F\norm A
        \longrightarrow0.
    \end{align*}
By the same argument as above, we find that
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{|t|\leq T}\norm{\tau_t^{s_nV}(A)-\tau_t^{sV}(A)}
        \longrightarrow0,\qquad A\in\mathcal{A}_\Gamma.
    \end{align*}
    This proves the continuity assertion. We now show that the convergence in $n$ is uniform in $s\in[0,1]$. If this failed, there would be a subsequence $n_j$ and parameters $s_j$ with a positive lower bound on
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{|t|\leq T}
        \lVert\tau_t^{s_jV_{n_j}}(A)-\tau_t^{s_jV}(A)\rVert.
    \end{align*}
    By compactness, we may pass to a further subsequence such that $s_j\to s$. We calculate
    \begin{align*}
        &\sup_{|t|\leq T}\norm{\tau_t^{s_jV_{n_j}}(A)-\tau_t^{s_jV}(A)}\\
        &\quad\leq
        \sup_{|t|\leq T}\norm{\tau_t^{s_jV_{n_j}}(A)-\tau_t^{sV}(A)}
        +\sup_{|t|\leq T}\norm{\tau_t^{s_jV}(A)-\tau_t^{sV}(A)}
        \longrightarrow0.
    \end{align*}
    The first term converges to zero by the preceding convergence result for the sequence $s_j\to s$, and the second term converges to zero by the continuity just proved. This contradiction proves uniform convergence.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{prop: LiebRobinson} 
    Let $\varPhi \in \mathcal{P}_{D + \alpha}$ be an interaction with $\alpha > D$ and let $\sigma \in ((D + 1)  / (\alpha + 1), 1)$ be arbitrary. Then $\mathcal{L}_{\varPhi}$ generates a unique group of automorphisms $\tau$. Furthermore, there exists a polynomially growing function $f \colon \mathbb{R}_+ \to \mathbb{R}_+$, such that $\tau$ satisfies a Lieb--Robinson bound of the form
    \begin{align*}
        \zeta_\mathrm{LR} (X, Y, |t|)
        \leq  \min( |X|, |Y|) \, f(\lvert t \rvert) \bigl(\exp \bigl( \min( 0, v \lvert t \rvert - d(X,Y)^{1 - \sigma }) \bigr) + (1 + d (X, Y))^{- \sigma \alpha} \bigr) \, .
    \end{align*}
    Moreover, for any $C > 0$, $f$ and $v > 0$ can be chosen uniformly for all $\varPhi$ with $ \lVert \varPhi \rVert_{D + \alpha} < C$.
    
    Independently, if $\varPhi\in\mathcal{P}_{\exp(-\mu\cdot)}$ for some $\mu>0$, then, for every $0<\lambda<\mu$, there are constants $C_{\mu,\lambda},\kappa_{\mu,\lambda}>0$, depending only on $\Gamma$, $\mu$ and $\lambda$, such that
    \begin{align*}
        \zeta_\mathrm{LR}(X,Y,|t|)
        &\leq C_{\mu,\lambda}\min (|X|, |Y| ) \, 
        \exp\bigl(\kappa_{\mu,\lambda}\lVert\varPhi\rVert_{\exp(-\mu\cdot)}|t|
                   -\lambda d(X,Y)\bigr).
    \end{align*}
    In particular, the coefficient of $|t|$ is linear in the interaction norm.
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
The existence of dynamics follows by standard Lieb--Robinson bounds. The result for polynomially decaying interactions is shown in \cite[Theorem 6]{TW25}. The result for exponentially decaying interactions follows from standard Lieb--Robinson bounds for short-ranged interactions; see, for instance, \cite[Theorem 4.3]{BP17}.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem: perturbed LRB}
    Let $H \in \mathcal{P}_\nu$ with $\nu > 2 D$ and $P = P^* \in \mathcal{A}_X^+$. Assume that $H$ generates a dynamics $\tau$ that satisfies a Lieb--Robinson bound with $\zeta_\mathrm{LR}$. Then the perturbed dynamics $\tau^P$ satisfies a Lieb--Robinson bound of the following type
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert [\tau_t^P (A), \, B] \rVert \leq \lVert A \rVert \, \lVert B \rVert \bigl( \zeta_\mathrm{LR} (Y, Z, |t|) + 2 |t| \, \lVert P \rVert \min_{W \in \{ Y, Z\}} \zeta_\mathrm{LR} (X, W, |t|)  \bigr) \, ,
    \end{align*}
    for all $A \in \mathcal{A}_Y^+$, $B \in \mathcal{A}_Z$ and $t \in \mathbb{R}$, provided that $t\mapsto\zeta_\mathrm{LR}(Y,Z,t)$ is non-decreasing for every $Y,Z\in P_0(\Gamma)$.
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
This statement was shown for finite lattices in \cite[Lemma 42]{CMTW25}. We give a derivation in our setting. To this end, we first compare the perturbed and unperturbed dynamics. Let $C \in \mathcal{A}_W$. By differentiation we find that
    \begin{align*}
        \tau_t^P(C)-\tau_t(C)
        &=\mathrm{i}\int_0^t\tau_s^P\bigl([P,\tau_{t-s}(C)]\bigr)\,\mathrm{d} s.
    \end{align*}
    Since $P$ is even, the Lieb--Robinson bound and its monotonicity in time imply that
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{\tau_t^P(C)-\tau_t(C)}
        &\leq |t|\sup_{|u|\leq|t|}\norm{[P,\tau_u(C)]}\\
        &=|t|\sup_{|u|\leq|t|}\norm{[\tau_{-u}(P),C]}\\
        &\leq |t|\norm P\norm C\,\zeta_\mathrm{LR}(X,W,|t|).
    \end{align*}
    Applying this estimate with $C=A$ gives
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{[\tau_t^P(A),B]}
        &\leq\norm{[\tau_t(A),B]}
        +2\norm B\norm{\tau_t^P(A)-\tau_t(A)}\\
        &\leq\norm A\norm B\bigl(
        \zeta_\mathrm{LR}(Y,Z,|t|)
        +2|t|\norm P\zeta_\mathrm{LR}(X,Y,|t|)\bigr).
    \end{align*}
    On the other hand, applying this identity to $C=B$, gives
    \begin{align*}
        \norm{[\tau_t^P(A),B]}
        &=\norm{[A,\tau_{-t}^P(B)]}\\
        &\leq\norm{[A,\tau_{-t}(B)]}
        +2\norm A\norm{\tau_{-t}^P(B)-\tau_{-t}(B)}\\
        &\leq\norm A\norm B\bigl(
        \zeta_\mathrm{LR}(Y,Z,|t|)
        +2|t|\norm P\zeta_\mathrm{LR}(X,Z,|t|)\bigr).
    \end{align*}
    Taking the minimum over the two bounds gives the statement.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
For $\beta=0$, the assertions about $\varPhi_\beta$ follow from $\varPhi_0=\mathrm{id}$. The bound on the dynamics is proved below and does not depend on $\beta$. In the calculations involving $f_\beta$, assume $\beta\neq0$.
    
    Let $k \in \mathbb{N}_0$ and $z \in \Gamma$. We denote by $\tau^{X, P}$ the dynamics generated by $\mathcal{L}_\varPsi + \mathcal{L}_{V_X + P}$, and proceed to bound
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_k (z)} ) \, \varPhi_\beta^{\tau^{X, P}} (A) \rVert & \leq
        \int_\mathbb{R} \mathrm{d} t \, f_\beta(t) \, \lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_k (z)}) \, \tau_{-t}^{X, P} (A) \rVert
        \\
        &\leq \sup_{|t| < T} \, \lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_k (z)}) \, \tau_{-t}^{X, P} (A) \rVert + 2 \, \lVert A \rVert \, \int_{|t| \geq T} \mathrm{d} t \, f_\beta (t) \, .
    \end{align*}
The explicit kernel \cite[Eq.~(26)]{CMTW25} gives
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{|t|\geq T}f_\beta(t)\,\mathrm{d} t
        &=\frac8{\pi^2}\sum_{j=0}^\infty
            \frac{\mathrm{e}^{-(2j+1)\pi T/|\beta|}}{(2j+1)^2}
        \leq\mathrm{e}^{-\pi T/|\beta|}.
    \end{align*}
    Hence
    \begin{align*} \label{eq: naive QBP locality}
        \int_\mathbb{R} \mathrm{d} t \, f_\beta(t) \, \lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_k (z)}) \, \tau_{-t}^{X, P} (A) \rVert \leq \sup_{|t| < T} \, \lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_k (z)}) \, \tau_{-t}^{X, P} (A) \rVert + 2 \, \mathrm{e}^{- \frac{\pi}{|\beta| } T } \, \lVert A \rVert  \, , \addtocounter{equation}{1}\tag{\theequation} 
    \end{align*}
    for any $T > 0$. We now proceed to bound $\lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_k (z)}) \, \tau_{-t}^{X, P} (A) \rVert$.
    In \cite[Lemma 2.10]{BTW26} similar bounds on the decay norm of dynamics with $V = 0$ and $P = 0$ are shown. We follow the idea of the proof. 
    
    We first define finite-volume approximations of $\varPsi$, $V$, and $P$. We set
    \begin{align*}
        \varPsi_{k}(M) \coloneq  \varPsi(M)   \, 
    \end{align*}
    for all $M \subseteq B_{k}(z)$  and $\varPsi_{k}(M) = 0 $ otherwise.
    Similarly, for $V$, we define the finite volume approximation $V_{X, k}$ of $V_X = \sum_{Z \subseteq X} V (Z)$ by
    \begin{align*}
        V_{X, k} (M) &\coloneq  V(M) \, ,
    \end{align*}
    for all $M \subseteq B_{k} (z) \cap X$ and $V_{X, k} (M) = 0$ otherwise. Notice that these choices ensure that $\lVert \varPsi_k \rVert_F \leq \lVert \varPsi \rVert_F$ and $\lVert V_{X, k} \rVert_F \leq \lVert V \rVert_F$.
    For the bounded perturbation $P$, we define the approximation $P_{ k}$ of $P$ near $z$ by
    \begin{align*}
        P_{k} &\coloneq \mathbb{E}_{B_{k / 4} (z)} \, P \, .
    \end{align*}
    We denote the automorphism generated by $\mathcal{L}_{\varPsi_k + V_{X, k}} + \mathcal{L}_{P_k}$ with $\tau^{X, P, k}$. Note that the automorphisms $\tau^{X, P, k}$ map $\mathcal{A}_{B_k(z)}$ to itself, since all local terms of $\varPsi_k$ and $V_{X, k}$ and $P_k$ lie in $\mathcal{A}_{B_k(z)}$.
    
    Let $A \in \mathcal{A}_F$ be arbitrary. We will begin to bound
    \begin{align*}
        \lVert (1-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(z)}) \, \tau_t^{X, P} (A) \rVert 
        \leq 2 \, \lVert (1 - \mathbb{E}_{B_{k / 4}(z)}) \, A \rVert + \lVert (1-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(z)}) \, \tau_t^{X, P} (\mathbb{E}_{B_{k / 4}(z)}\, A )\rVert   \, 
    \end{align*}
    by something that decays as $F(k)$ in $k$. This bound will be sufficient for short times. For long times we will use the trivial bound $\lVert (1-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(z)}) \, \tau_t^{X, P} (A) \rVert  \leq 2 \, \lVert A \rVert$ and the decay in $|t|$ of the integral. The first term is bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        2\norm{(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)})A}
        &\leq2F(\lfloor k/4\rfloor)\norm A_{F,z}
        \leq\frac{2}{F(1)}F(k/4)\norm A_{F,z}.
    \end{align*} 
    For the second term, we use the fundamental theorem of calculus and find
    \begin{align*}
        &\norm{(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(z)})\tau_t^{X,P}(\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A)}\\
        &\quad=\norm{(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(z)})
                      (\tau_t^{X,P}-\tau_t^{X,P,k})\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A}\\
        &\quad\leq2\norm{(\tau_t^{X,P}-\tau_t^{X,P,k})\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A}\\
        &\quad\leq2\int_{\min(0,t)}^{\max(0,t)}
                  \norm{\partial_s(\tau_s^{X,P}\tau_{t-s}^{X,P,k})
                             \mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A}\,\mathrm{d} s\\
        &\quad=2\int_{\min(0,t)}^{\max(0,t)}
           \norm{(\mathcal{L}_\varPsi+\mathcal{L}_{V_X+P}-\mathcal{L}_{\varPsi_k+V_{X,k}}-\mathcal{L}_{P_k})
                    \tau_{t-s}^{X,P,k}(\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A)}\,\mathrm{d} s.
    \end{align*}
    Note that $\tau_{t - s}^{X, P, k} (\mathbb{E}_{B_{{k / 4}}(z)} \, A)$ is local and therefore lies in the domain of $\mathcal{L}_{\varPsi} + \mathcal{L}_{V_X + P}$. By Lemma \ref{lem: derivations} and the triangle inequality,
    \begin{align*}
        &\norm{(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(z)})\tau_t^{X,P}(\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A)}\\
        &\quad\leq2\int_{\min(0,t)}^{\max(0,t)}\mathrm{d} s
          \sum_{\substack{Z\cap B_{k/2}(z)\neq\emptyset\\Z\cap B_k(z)^c\neq\emptyset}}
          \norm{[\varPsi(Z)+\mathbf1_{Z\subseteq X}V(Z),
                       \tau_{t-s}^{X,P,k}(\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A)]}\\
        &\qquad+2\int_{\min(0,t)}^{\max(0,t)}\mathrm{d} s
          \sum_{\substack{Z\cap B_{k/2}(z)=\emptyset\\Z\cap B_k(z)^c\neq\emptyset}}
          \norm{[\varPsi(Z)+\mathbf1_{Z\subseteq X}V(Z),
                       \tau_{t-s}^{X,P,k}(\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A)]}\\
        &\qquad+2\int_{\min(0,t)}^{\max(0,t)}
          \norm{[(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)})P,
                       \tau_{t-s}^{X,P,k}(\mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A)]}\,\mathrm{d} s.
    \end{align*}
    All sums run over $Z\in P_0(\Gamma)$. The last term is bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        \frac4{F(1)}|t|F(k/4)\norm P_{F,z}\norm A.
    \end{align*}
    Let $G$ be a decay function, chosen below, such that
    \begin{align*}
        \sup_{r\geq0}(1+r)^DG(r/2)<\infty,\qquad
        \sum_{r=0}^\infty(1+r)^{D-1}G(r/4)<\infty.
    \end{align*}
    Every term in the first sum has diameter at least $k/2$. This sum, including the time integral, is therefore bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        &4|t|\norm A\,|B_{k/2}(z)|F(k/2)G(k/2)
                   (\norm\varPsi_{FG}+\norm V_{FG})\\
        &\quad\leq4C|t|F(k/2)(\norm\varPsi_{FG}+\norm V_{FG})\norm A,
    \end{align*}
    where $C$ depends only on the lattice and $G$.

    For the second sum, choose $x\in Z\cap B_k(z)^c$ and insert the conditional expectation onto $B_{d(x,z)/4}(x)$ in each interaction term. The part outside this ball vanishes unless $\mathrm{diam} Z>d(x,z)/4$. Its contribution is bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        &8|t|\norm A\sum_{x\in B_k(z)^c}
          \sum_{\substack{Z\ni x\\\mathrm{diam} Z>d(x,z)/4}}
                \bigl(\norm{\varPsi(Z)}+\mathbf1_{Z\subseteq X}\norm{V(Z)}\bigr)\\
        &\quad\leq8|t|F(k/4)(\norm\varPsi_{FG}+\norm V_{FG})
                      \norm A\sum_{x\in B_k(z)^c}G(d(x,z)/4)\\
        &\quad\leq C^\prime|t|F(k/4)
                     (\norm\varPsi_{FG}+\norm V_{FG})\norm A.
    \end{align*}
    Here $C^\prime$ depends only on the lattice and $G$. For the local part, invariance of the norm under the dynamics and Lemma \ref{lem: perturbed LRB} give
    \begin{align*}
        &2\int_{\min(0,t)}^{\max(0,t)}\mathrm{d} s
          \sum_{x\in B_k(z)^c}
          \sum_{\substack{Z\ni x\\Z\cap B_{k/2}(z)=\emptyset}}
          \norm{[\tau_{s-t}^{X,P,k}
             (\mathbb{E}_{B_{d(x,z)/4}(x)}(\varPsi(Z)+\mathbf1_{Z\subseteq X}V(Z))),
                         \mathbb{E}_{B_{k/4}(z)}A]}\\
        &\quad\leq2|t|\norm A(1+2|t|\norm P)
          \sum_{x\in B_k(z)^c}\zeta_\mathrm{LR}(B_{d(x,z)/4}(x),B_{k/4}(z),|t|)\\
        &\qquad\qquad\times\sum_{Z\ni x}
                    \bigl(\norm{\varPsi(Z)}+\mathbf1_{Z\subseteq X}\norm{V(Z)}\bigr)\\
        &\quad\leq2|t|\norm A(1+2|t|\norm P)
          (\norm\varPsi_{FG}+\norm V_{FG})
          \sum_{x\in B_k(z)^c}\zeta_\mathrm{LR}(B_{d(x,z)/4}(x),B_{k/4}(z),|t|).
          \addtocounter{equation}{1}\tag{\theequation}\label{eq: locality QBP LRB}
    \end{align*}
    It remains to bound $\zeta_\mathrm{LR} \bigl( B_{d(x, z) / 4}(x), \, B_{k / 4} (z), |t| \bigr)$. Here we use the fact that the distance of the supports is at least $3 d(x, z) / 4 - k /4$. For $d(x, z) > k$ this is at least $k / 4$ and at least $d(x, z) / 4$. We distinguish two cases: polynomially and exponentially decaying interactions.
    


    
    \textbf{Polynomial decay.} Choose $\varepsilon>0$ such that $3D+\nu+\varepsilon<\mu$, and set
    $F(r)=(1+r)^{-\nu}$ and $G(r)=(1+r)^{-3D-\varepsilon}$. We choose $\sigma$ > 0 such that
    \begin{align*}
        \max  \bigl( \frac{D+1}{2D+\nu+\varepsilon+1},
        \frac{D+\nu}{D+\nu+\varepsilon/2}
        \bigr )<\sigma<1.
    \end{align*}
    We also set $\delta \coloneq \sigma(D+\varepsilon/2)-D > 0$. Proposition \ref{prop: LiebRobinson} gives
    \begin{align*}
        &\zeta_\mathrm{LR}(B_{d(x,z)/4}(x),B_{k/4}(z),|t|)\\
        &\quad\leq C_\mathrm{vol} (1+k / 4)^D f(|t|)
        \Bigl(\exp\bigl(\min (0,v|t|-(k/8+d(x,z)/8)^{1-\sigma})\bigr)\\
        &\hspace{17em} +(1+k/8+d(x,z)/8)^{-\sigma(2D+\nu+\varepsilon)}\Bigr).
    \end{align*}
    For the second summand we calculate
    \begin{align*}
        (1+k/8+d(x,z)/8)^{-\sigma(2D+\nu+\varepsilon)} &\leq
        (1+k/8)^{-\sigma(D+\nu+\varepsilon/2)}
        (1+d(x,z)/8)^{-\sigma(D+\varepsilon/2)}\\
        &\leq
        (1+k/8)^{-D-\nu}(1+d(x,z)/8)^{-D-\delta}.
    \end{align*}
    We notice that for any $a$, $b \geq 0$ it holds that 
    \begin{align*}
        1 + \max (0, a - b) \geq \frac{1+ a}{ 1 + b} .
    \end{align*}
    We choose $\alpha>2\max (D+\nu,D+\delta)/(1-\sigma)$. Since
    $\exp(\min(0,b-a))=\exp(-\max(0,a-b))$, we calculate
    \[
    \begin{aligned}
        &\exp(\min (0, v|t|-(k/8+d(x,z)/8)^{1-\sigma} ) )\\
        &\quad\leq C_\alpha
        \bigl(1+\max(0,(k/8+d(x,z)/8)^{1-\sigma}-v|t|)\bigr)^{-\alpha}\\
        &\quad\leq C_\alpha(1+v|t|)^\alpha
        \bigl(1+(k/8+d(x,z)/8)^{1-\sigma}\bigr)^{-\alpha},
    \end{aligned}
    \]
    for some constant $C_\alpha > 0$. Moreover,
    \[
    \begin{aligned}
      &\bigl(1+(k/8+d(x,z)/8)^{1-\sigma}\bigr)^{-\alpha}\\
      &\quad\leq
      \bigl(1+(k/8)^{1-\sigma}\bigr)^{-\alpha/2}
      \bigl(1+(d(x,z)/8)^{1-\sigma}\bigr)^{-\alpha/2}\\
      &\quad\leq 8^{\alpha (1 - \sigma)}
      (1+k)^{-\alpha(1-\sigma)/2}
      (1+d(x,z))^{-\alpha(1-\sigma)/2}\\
      &\quad\leq 8^{\alpha (1 - \sigma)}
      (1+k)^{-D-\nu}(1+d(x,z))^{-D-\delta},
    \end{aligned}
    \]
    Hence, for some polynomial $\widetilde f$,
    \begin{align*}
        &\sum_{x\in B_k(z)^c}
        \zeta_\mathrm{LR}(B_{d(x,z)/4}(x),B_{k/4}(z),|t|) \leq C \widetilde f(|t|)(1+k)^{-\nu},
    \end{align*}
    where $C>0$ depends only on the surface-regularity constants and the fixed exponents $\alpha$, $\sigma$, $\nu$ and $\delta$. Combining all bounds, we get 
    \begin{gather*}
        \lVert  \tau_t^{X, P} (A) \rVert_{\nu, z} \leq f^\prime (|t|) \,  \, \lVert A \rVert_{\nu, z} \, \bigl( 1 + \lVert P \rVert_{ \nu, z} \bigr)   \, 
        \bigl( 1 + \lVert \varPsi \rVert_{\nu + 3D + \varepsilon} + \lVert V \rVert_{\nu + 3D + \varepsilon} \bigr) \,  \, ,
    \end{gather*}
    where $f^\prime$ is some polynomial. This shows the first claim. To obtain the stated temperature dependence, use
    $f_\beta(t)=|\beta|^{-1}f_1(t/|\beta|)$. If
    $f^\prime(r)=\sum_{j=0}^N c_jr^j$, then
    \[
    \begin{aligned}
      \int_\mathbb{R} f_\beta(t)f^\prime(|t|)\,\mathrm{d} t
      &=\sum_{j=0}^Nc_j|\beta|^j
        \int_\mathbb{R} f_1(u)|u|^j\,\mathrm{d} u.
    \end{aligned}
    \]
    All moments on the right-hand side are finite because $f_1$ decays exponentially. Hence this expression is a polynomial in $|\beta|$, which proves the second claim in the long-range regime.

    \textbf{Exponential decay.} We choose $F(r) = \exp(-\min (b, c/2)r)$ and $G(r) = \exp(-cr/2)$ and define $I \coloneq \lVert \varPsi \rVert_{\exp(- c \cdot)} + \lVert V \rVert_{\exp(-c \cdot)}$. We use the Lieb--Robinson bound from Proposition \ref{prop: LiebRobinson} with $\mu=c$ and $\lambda=c/2$. We obtain the bound
    \begin{align*}
        &\zeta_\mathrm{LR}\bigl(B_{d(x,z)/4}(x),B_{k/4}(z),|t|\bigr) \leq C(1+k)^D \exp\left(\kappa_{c,c/2}I|t|-\frac{ck}{8} -\frac{c\,d(x,z)}8\right),
    \end{align*}
    for some constants $C$, $\kappa_{c,c/2} > 0$ that are independent of $x$, $z$, $k$ and $t$. Therefore, the expression in Equation \eqref{eq: locality QBP LRB} is bounded by
    \begin{align*}
        C'I|t|\norm A(1+2|t|\norm P)(1+k)^D
          \exp\left(\kappa_{c,c/2}I|t|-\frac{ck}{8}\right),
    \end{align*}
    for a constant $C^\prime > 0$ that is independent of $x$, $z$, $k$ and $t$. Combining this with the preceding bounds gives
    \begin{align*}
        &\norm{(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(z)})\tau_t^{X,P}(A)}\\
        &\quad\leq
        C^{\prime \prime}\norm A_{\exp(-b\cdot),z}
        (1+\norm P_{\exp(-b\cdot),z})\\
        &\qquad\times\left((1+|t|)\exp(-\min (b,c/2 )k/4)
        +I|t|(1+|t|)(1+k)^D
          \exp\left(\kappa_{c,c/2}I|t|-\frac{ck}{8}\right)\right),
    \end{align*}
    for a constant $C^{\prime \prime } > 0$ that is independent of $x$, $z$, $k$ and $t$. We choose
    \begin{align*}
        C_\mathrm{int}(a,c)\coloneq\frac{\pi}{2\kappa_{c,c/2}}
            \left(\frac c{8a}-1\right),
        \qquad
        T\coloneq\frac{c|\beta|k}{8(\pi+\kappa_{c,c/2}I|\beta|)}.
    \end{align*}
    Since $c>8a$, we have $C_\mathrm{int}(a,c)>0$. For $k\geq1$, the two time-dependent exponents satisfy
    \begin{align*}
        \kappa_{c,c/2}IT-\frac{ck}{8}
        &=-\frac{\pi T}{|\beta|}
        =-\frac{c\pi k}{8(\pi+\kappa_{c,c/2}I|\beta|)}
        \leq-\frac{2ac}{c+8a}k,
    \end{align*}
    where we used $I|\beta|<C_\mathrm{int}(a,c)$. Moreover,
    \begin{align*}
        T&\leq\frac{c|\beta|k}{8\pi},\\
        1+T&\leq\left(1+\frac c{8\pi}\right)(1+|\beta|)(1+k),\\
        IT(1+T)&\leq\frac{cC_\mathrm{int}(a,c)}{8\pi}
                 \left(1+\frac c{8\pi}\right)(1+|\beta|)(1+k)^2.
    \end{align*}
    Using these bounds in Equation \eqref{eq: naive QBP locality} gives
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_\mathbb{R} f_\beta(t)
          \norm{(1-\mathbb{E}_{B_k(z)})\tau_{-t}^{X,P}(A)}\,\mathrm{d} t\\
        &\quad\leq C^{\prime\prime\prime}(1+|\beta|)
             \norm A_{\exp(-b\cdot),z}(1+\norm P_{\exp(-b\cdot),z})\\
        &\qquad\times\left((1+k)\mathrm{e}^{-\min(b,c/2)k/4}
                     +(1+k)^{D+2}\mathrm{e}^{-2ac k/(c+8a)}\right).
    \end{align*}
    The constant $C^{\prime\prime\prime}$ is independent of $x$, $z$, $k$ and $\beta$. Since $\min(b,c/2)/4>a$ and $2ac/(c+8a)>a$, multiplication by $\mathrm{e}^{ak}$ leaves a quantity bounded uniformly in $k$. The term $k=0$ is bounded by $2\norm A$. Adding the operator norm proves the exponential statement.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
